O050Osmani

User-centric engineering.

Addy Osmani · 2024-11-15

Customer needs and observable outcomes—not lines of code—should determine whether an engineering change succeeds.

Engineering structure

Related practices

PAT:059 Measurement & LearningInforms practice · primary reading
Why these practices (1)
  • PAT:059 · Informs practice

    Customer needs and observable outcomes directly inform the unit used to judge an engineering change instead of lines of code. These general product principles do not supply an original AI effectiveness evaluation.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Evidence, context & provenance
Evidence boundaryGeneral user-experience and product principles, no original AI evaluation.

Recorded summary. Osmani argues that engineering decisions should start with user needs and observable benefit rather than technical elegance. He offers questions to ask before development, during iterative implementation and after launch, supported by practices such as support-ticket analysis, behavioral observation, interviews and product feedback. The article does not evaluate AI coding systems.

Reported findings / argument. Start with a concrete user problem and a user-observable success criterion; Validate design trade-offs through user interviews, usage data, and support feedback; Use only as background product engineering context, not as evidence of AI productivity.

Source type, as recorded: Practitioner essay / technical guidance (not peer-reviewed)

Editorial use. Start with a concrete user problem and a user-observable success criterion; Validate design trade-offs through user interviews, usage data, and support feedback; Use only as background product engineering context, not as evidence of AI productivity.

O051Osmani

Agentic Verification

Addy Osmani · 2026-10-14

Reliable agent work depends on executable checks: realistic user flows, explicit properties and differential comparisons, with fast feedback and human-owned success criteria.

Verification & reviewCode quality & security

Related practices

PAT:049 Runtime-in-the-LoopInforms practice · primary readingPAT:027 Maker–Checker: Separate Production from AcceptanceCautions about practice
Why these practices (2)
  • PAT:049 · Informs practice

    Realistic user flows, explicit properties and differential comparisons inform which runtime observations should influence the next development decision. The guidance does not establish universal correctness outside the paths and properties actually checked.

    Explore this practice with its sources →
  • PAT:027 · Cautions about practice

    The explicit warning about generated tests and oracle independence cautions against allowing maker-produced checks to become self-approval. It identifies a verification boundary without showing that any proposed checker is independent.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Evidence, context & provenance
Evidence boundaryPractitioner guidance, not a controlled experiment or proof of universal correctness; generated tests and oracle independence still need scrutiny.

Recorded summary. Osmani recommends autonomous feedback loops using end-to-end checks, property-based testing and old/new behavioral comparisons. Checks should run quickly and deterministically, preserve actionable failures, and be grounded in success criteria the human owns.

Source type, as recorded: Practitioner essay / technical guidance (not peer-reviewed)

Editorial use. Define the oracle before implementation; retain failing inputs; inspect generated tests; run heavier integration checks before release.

Your parallel Agent limit

Addy Osmani · 2026-04-07

Osmani argues that parallel agent work is constrained by a person’s capacity to understand, evaluate and integrate separate tasks. He recommends adjusting concurrency to task complexity and review quality, defining time limits and acceptance criteria, and reducing per-task scope when supervision becomes shallow.

Engineering structureProductivitySkills & learning

Related practices

PAT:056 Parallel Work OrchestrationCautions about practice · primary reading
Why these practices (1)
  • PAT:056 · Cautions about practice

    The limit imposed by understanding, evaluation and integration capacity informs reducing concurrency when supervision becomes shallow. Personal heuristics about agent counts and fatigue are not a validated scaling law.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Agentic Engine Optimization (AEO)

Addy Osmani · 2026-04-11

Osmani proposes adapting developer documentation and interfaces to agents that fetch and consume content directly. His recommendations emphasize discoverable, concise, machine-readable documentation, clear capability descriptions and server-side observation of usage. He presents documentation indexes, skills and tool interfaces as possible components rather than a single solution.

System knowledge & memoryWhat agents can do

Related practices

PAT:028 Skills and Context Engineering: Remove Intent DebtInforms practice · primary readingPAT:030 Connectors and Tools: Move Exact Work Outside the WeightsInforms practice
Why these practices (2)
  • PAT:028 · Informs practice

    Discoverable, concise documentation and selective skills inform packaging reusable procedural knowledge for an agent without assuming one format solves every context problem. Proposed formats and token targets are not universal standards or demonstrated coding gains.

    Explore this practice with its sources →
  • PAT:030 · Informs practice

    Clear capability descriptions and tool interfaces inform how a connector exposes operations that an agent can select and an executor can check. Agent-traffic observations do not establish that those interfaces improve engineering outcomes.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Evidence, context & provenance
Evidence boundaryPractitioner proposal, explicitly not a Google-wide recommendation. Proposed formats and token targets are not universal standards; reported agent-traffic findings come from another study and do not establish improved coding outcomes.

Source type, as recorded: Theory / argument (not a new result)

Agent Harness Engineering

Addy Osmani · 2026-04-19

The article treats an agent’s tools, execution environment, durable state, context policies, checks and recovery paths as engineering artifacts surrounding the model. Osmani recommends deriving these controls from desired behavior and observed failures, and using deterministic enforcement and structured handoffs where prose instructions are insufficient.

Cognitive Surrender

Addy Osmani · 2026-05-05

Osmani distinguishes delegating execution while retaining judgment from adopting an AI answer without forming an independent view. He describes how this can appear in review, debugging and design, and recommends stating expectations, examining alternative explanations, requiring concrete verification evidence and keeping changes small enough to understand.

  • PAT:046 · Informs practice

    Keeping changes small enough to understand informs selecting a bounded review question before accepting an AI proposal. The recommendation addresses comprehension burden but does not establish a universal size threshold.

O057OsmaniPrimary text checked

Don't Outsource the Learning

Addy Osmani · 2026-05-16

The article separates completing programming tasks from learning the concepts needed to supervise future work. Osmani recommends forming a hypothesis before requesting a fix, asking for explanations before code, reviewing suggestions and periodically reconstructing solutions without assistance. He uses existing research to motivate deliberate learning within ordinary AI-assisted development.

Skills & learningSystem knowledge & memory

Related practices

PAT:050 Learning Loop PreservationInforms practice · primary reading
Why these practices (1)
  • PAT:050 · Informs practice

    Forming hypotheses, requesting explanations and periodically reconstructing solutions unaided inform preserving independent reasoning within assisted work. The mixed-population research synthesis does not establish long-term causal skill loss among developers.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Evidence, context & provenance
Evidence boundaryPractitioner synthesis of studies with different tasks and populations, including non-programming work; it does not establish long-term causal skill loss among software developers. Cited numerical findings were not independently rechecked here.

O058OsmaniPrimary text checked

The Orchestration Tax

Addy Osmani · 2026-05-24

Osmani describes the human cost of coordinating parallel coding agents as a limit on review and integration. He recommends matching the number of active tasks to review capacity, separating bounded background work from judgment-intensive problems, batching reviews and reserving uninterrupted time for difficult decisions. The goal is to improve accepted work rather than merely increase concurrent activity.

Engineering structureProductivityVerification & review

Related practices

PAT:056 Parallel Work OrchestrationCautions about practice · primary readingPAT:060 Cost ManagementInforms practice
Why these practices (2)
  • PAT:056 · Cautions about practice

    The coordination cost and scarce review time identify when additional parallel agents should be withheld or reduced. Illustrative agent counts and workload splits are not measured optima.

    Explore this practice with its sources →
  • PAT:060 · Informs practice

    The human work of coordinating, reviewing and integrating parallel tasks informs including those costs when comparing execution routes. The first-person account does not establish a general return on orchestration.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Evidence, context & provenance
Evidence boundaryFirst-person operational guidance and a concurrency analogy, not a measured agent-scaling law or controlled productivity experiment. Suggested agent counts and percentage splits are illustrative.

Source type, as recorded: Theory / argument (not a new result)

O059OsmaniPrimary text checked

The Intent Debt

Addy Osmani · 2026-06-05

The essay distinguishes missing recorded intent from complex code and missing human understanding. Osmani argues that an implementation cannot reliably recover why a consequential choice was made. He recommends keeping goals, constraints and decision rationale in specifications, repository instructions, lightweight decision logs and records of failures so future people and agents can use them.

System knowledge & memoryEngineering structure

Related practices

PAT:062 Research Artifact PreservationInforms practice · primary reading
Why these practices (1)
  • PAT:062 · Informs practice

    Preserving goals, constraints, decision rationale and failure records informs retaining the evidence needed to reconstruct why a system choice was made. Generated explanations cannot establish historical intent, and the proposal is not a controlled drift-prevention result.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Evidence, context & provenance
Evidence boundaryConceptual distinction and practitioner recommendations; no controlled evidence that recording these artifacts prevents drift, and generated explanations cannot establish historical intent.

The New Software Lifecycle

Addy Osmani · 2026-06-16

Osmani comments on a Google whitepaper he coauthored, presenting coding agents as models surrounded by tools, context, controls and feedback. He distinguishes always-loaded from task-specific context and separates evaluation of final outputs from evaluation of execution paths. He argues that specification and verification become central constraints as implementation accelerates.

Engineering structureVerification & reviewSystem knowledge & memory

The Agent-Era Career

Addy Osmani · 2026-07-06

Osmani’s career advice emphasizes choosing worthwhile problems, developing and demonstrating judgment, and retaining responsibility for delegated work. He recommends deliberate practice, explicit task boundaries, calibrated autonomy and careful completion beyond an agent’s initial draft. The article treats reputation and problem selection as sources of durable career value.

Skills & learningJobs & roles

Related practices

PAT:034 Safety and Guardrails: Verification Is Not AuthorizationInforms practice · primary reading
Why these practices (1)
  • PAT:034 · Informs practice

    Retaining responsibility, explicit task boundaries and calibrated delegation informs deciding which actions an agent may perform and who owns completion. Career advice and illustrative completion percentages do not validate an autonomy policy.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Evidence, context & provenance
Evidence boundaryPersonal experience and prospective career argument, not a labor-market study or validated prediction of which engineering tasks will be automated. Illustrative completion percentages are not measurements.

Source type, as recorded: Theory / argument (not a new result)

O062OsmaniPrimary text checked

Earning taste and judgment

Addy Osmani · 2026-07-14

Osmani argues that delegating routine coding can remove practice through which engineers develop judgment. He recommends deliberate code reading, recording agent mistakes, occasional unaided implementation, deep work in one system, and separate practice in specification and verification. The article connects these habits to career development while synthesizing other authors’ employment and cognition claims.

Skills & learningJobs & roles

Related practices

PAT:050 Learning Loop PreservationInforms practice · primary readingPAT:007 Compounding EngineeringInforms practice
Why these practices (2)
  • PAT:050 · Informs practice

    Unaided implementation, deliberate code reading and separate specification/verification practice inform preserving the reasoning that routine delegation can remove. The guidance does not constitute a controlled learning intervention or a new employment estimate.

    Explore this practice with its sources →
  • PAT:007 · Informs practice

    Recording agent mistakes informs turning completed-work experience into reusable guidance with context and limitations. This proposed habit does not establish that every recorded lesson generalizes or improves later performance.

    Explore this practice with its sources →

Primary reading is the main classification for this record, not a strength-of-evidence score. Associations use retained Atlas annotations; they add no manuscript citation or claim that the complete practice was validated.

Evidence, context & provenance
Evidence boundaryCareer and learning guidance, with secondhand labor-market statistics and forecasts; no new employment estimate or controlled learning intervention. Numerical claims are not independently revalidated here.

Source type, as recorded: Theory / argument (not a new result)
